For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Main** (ARR July cycle, ~July 15-25 → commit EMNLP Aug/Sep)\\n- Fallback: AAAI 2027 (abstract July 25, paper Aug 1)\\n\\n## Core Insight\\n\\nLLM-based document-level IE 已逼近 encoder SOTA 的抽取精度，但零方法能生成可验证证据。本文提出 **evidence-grounded DocRE** 作为新任务范式：LLM 同时输出关系三元组和支撑证据句。\\n\\n我们系统性地探索了 online RL (GRPO) 、offline preference optimization (DPO) 和 offline RL (RSFT) 三条技术路线。关键发现：**RL optimization pressure 与 evidence-extraction decoupling severity 正相关**——优化强度越大，evidence 质量提升的同时抽取能力退化越严重。QLoRA 放大至 catastrophic（rel_f1<0.02），bf16 GRPO 从 0.4549 退化至 ~0.434（mild, -2pp），DPO 呈 gradual 退化（1ep→3ep rel_f1 从 0.4034→0.3429），RSFT 无 decoupling。\\n\\n**Evidence tax 是 scale-dependent 的（non-monotonic pattern, D079/D082/D083）**：1.7B 模型中 evidence generation 反而带来 +6.2pp 收益（隐式正则化），4B 模型付出 -7.5pp 代价，8B Qwen 模型容量足够 absorb（≈0），8B LLaMA 跨家族确认（-0.7pp）。这推翻了\\\"evidence 总有代价\\\"的简单叙事。\\n\\nCED (Contrastive Evidence Discrimination) 是一种 NLI-based 候选筛选函数，通过文档内 hard negative 对比评估 evidence 质量。CED 和 flat-NLI 作为 RSFT scoring functions 统计不可区分（D078 paired t-test p=0.44），但均显著优于 random/no scoring。CED 因可解释性优势作为默认推荐方案。\\n\\n## Story Arc\\n\\n1. **Problem**: LLM DocRE 无证据 → 部署障碍\\n2. **New task**: evidence-grounded DocRE — 同时生成关系和证据\\n3. **Cost of faithfulness**: evidence generation 代价是 **scale-dependent** 的——4B 模型付出 -7.5pp 代价，但 1.7B 反而获益 +6.2pp，8B 趋近于零。这不是固定成本，而是与模型容量的非线性交互（D079/D082/D083）\\n4. **Exploration**: 系统性比较 online RL (GRPO) vs offline preference (DPO) vs offline RL (RSFT)\\n5. **Key finding**: evidence-extraction decoupling — RL optimization pressure 与 decoupling severity 正相关，形成连续谱系（GRPO-QLoRA catastrophic > GRPO-bf16 mild > DPO gradual > RSFT none）\\n6. **QLoRA amplification**: QLoRA 量化将 mild decoupling 放大至 catastrophic forgetting——decoupling 是 RL-for-IE 中 multi-objective competition 的通用梯度（intra-task trade-off，非 inter-task forgetting），QLoRA 是 catastrophic amplifier\\n7. **What works**: RSFT with NLI-based scoring 是唯一有效路线——提升 evidence 质量而不牺牲抽取\\n8. **Best practical solution**: CED reranker（response-level 选择）恢复 97% 的 evidence 代价，evi_f1=0.8188。注意：CED 有效粒度是 response-level reranking，不是 sentence-level filtering（D090: sentence-level CED filter 是 negative result，recall -20pp）\\n8b. **Evidence strategy spectrum (D090/D091)**: Post-hoc retrieval（加法，BM25 k=2 evi_f1_joint=0.347）< Joint generation（RSFT, evi_f1_joint=0.361）。Post-hoc filter（减法，Lead 1 CED sentence filter）= negative。Joint generation 产出的 evidence 更 faithful（EDCR 0.654 vs 0.766）\\n9. **Diagnostic tool**: EDCR 双层诊断——方法级量化 decoupling（与 Claim 2 呼应），per-relation 级揭示 evi_f1 不捕捉的 relation-type 难度差异（D070 ✅）\\n10. **Scaling & generalization**: evidence tax 呈 non-monotonic scaling pattern（D079/D082/D083），跨模型家族（LLaMA-8B, D082）和跨数据集（Re-DocRED, \\\"generative LLM\\\" 限定词——SAIS 已做 encoder-based evidence retrieval + DocRE）\\n- **关键区分 (Reviewer warning)**：encoder-based evidence *retrieval*（SAIS, DREEAM — 分类哪些句子是证据，evidence supervision 提升 RE）vs (Limitations): GenRES 指出 exact match F1 低估 generative RE，FREIGE 所有 eval 用 exact match，应承认此局限（对所有方法同等适用，相对比较有效）\\n\\n### Claim 2 (Core finding): RL optimization pressure 与 evidence-extraction 的通用梯度**: 跨方法（GRPO/DPO）、跨量化策略（QLoRA/bf16）一致出现，严重程度与优化压力正相关\\n- 三档 Dream 审稿均认为此发现\\\\\\\"genuinely novel and striking\\\\\\\"\\n- **已完成**: GRPO G=8 bf16 确认 mild decoupling（plan_011 ✅）; DPO 1ep/3ep 确认 训练集规模: k=1 选出 2718 样本 (12.4%), k=3 选出 8037 样本 (37%), 数据量 ×2.96 但质量下降抵消数量优势\\n  - **叙事**: 低排名 response 引入噪声——rank 2-3 样本的 F1 和 CED 分数均显著低于 rank 1，扩大训练集规模无法补偿数据质量的稀释。这表明 RSFT 的关键不在数据量而在 scoring 不捕捉的 relation-type 诊断信息 ✅\\n  - per-relation ρ(EDCR, evi_f1) = **-0.064** (p=0.58) → **完全独立于 evi_f1**\\n  - per-relation ρ(EDCR, rel_f1) = -0.793 (p<0.001) → 与 rel_f1 强相关但非完美\\n  - per-relation ρ(evi_f1, rel_f1) = -0.013 (p=0.91) → evidence 质量与抽取能力在 relation type 间完全解耦\\n  - 典型案例：date_of_birth (EDCR=0.118, 高 faithful) vs head_of_government (EDCR=0.956, 高 decoupled)\\n  - 93 relation types 分析（≥5 样本），76 个有非零数据\\n- **论文叙事**: EDCR 在方法级是 decoupling 度量工具（横向对比方法），在 relation 级是难度诊断工具（纵向定位薄弱 relation）——两层叙事互补\\n- **仍需**: 100 例人工标注验证（RC6）\\n\\n### Claim 5 (NEW — Scaling): Evidence Tax 呈 公平子集分析确认）\\n  - **4B (Qwen3-4B)**: -7.5pp cost — 标准 evidence generation 负担，模型容量不足以同时优化两个目标\\n  - **8B Qwen (Qwen3-8B)**: ≈0 — 模型容量足够 absorb evidence generation 的额外负担\\n  - **8B LLaMA (LLaMA-3.1-8B)**: -0.7pp — 跨家族确认，8B 规模的 evidence tax 趋近于零\\n- **Non-monotonic 解释**: 小模型从 evidence 的正则化效应获益 > 生成负担；中等模型容量不足以同时做好两件事；大模型容量充裕，两个目标互不干扰\\n- **论文叙事**: 推翻 \\\"evidence 总有代价\\\" 的简单叙事，揭示 evidence generation (Contrastive Evidence Discrimination) 是一种 NLI-based 候选筛选函数，通过文档内 hard negative 对比评估 evidence 质量。CED 和 flat-NLI 作为 RSFT scoring functions 统计不可区分（D078 paired t-test p=0.44），但均显著优于 random/no scoring。CED 因可解释性优势作为默认推荐方案。\\\\n\\\\n## Story Arc\\\\n\\\\n1. **Problem**: LLM DocRE 无证据 → 部署障碍\\\\n2. **New task**: evidence-grounded DocRE — 同时生成关系和证据\\\\n3. **Cost of faithfulness**: evidence generation 代价是 **scale-dependent** 的——4B 模型付出 -7.5pp 代价，但 1.7B 反而获益 +6.2pp，8B 趋近于零。这不是固定成本，而是与模型容量的非线性交互（D079/D082/D083）\\\\n4. **Exploration**: 系统性比较 online RL (GRPO) vs offline preference (DPO) vs offline RL (RSFT)\\\\n5. **Key finding**: evidence-extraction decoupling — RL optimization pressure 与 decoupling severity 正相关，形成连续谱系（GRPO-QLoRA catastrophic > GRPO-bf16 mild > DPO gradual > RSFT none）\\\\n6. **QLoRA amplification**: QLoRA 量化将 mild decoupling 放大至 catastrophic forgetting——decoupling 是 RL-for-IE 中 multi-objective competition 的通用梯度（intra-task trade-off，非 inter-task forgetting），QLoRA 是 catastrophic amplifier\\\\n7. **What works**: RSFT with NLI-based scoring 是唯一有效路线——提升 evidence 质量而不牺牲抽取\\\\n8. **Best practical solution**: CED reranker（response-level 选择）恢复 97% 的 evidence 代价，evi_f1=0.8188。注意：CED 有效粒度是 response-level reranking，不是 sentence-level filtering（D090: sentence-level CED filter 是 negative result，recall -20pp）\\\\n8b. **Evidence strategy spectrum (D090/D091)**: Post-hoc retrieval（加法，BM25 k=2 evi_f1_joint=0.347）< Joint generation（RSFT, evi_f1_joint=0.361）。Post-hoc filter（减法，Lead 1 CED sentence filter）= negative。Joint generation 产出的 evidence 更 faithful（EDCR 0.654 vs 0.766）\\\\n9. **Diagnostic tool**: EDCR 双层诊断——方法级量化 decoupling（与 Claim 2 呼应），per-relation 级揭示 evi_f1 不捕捉的 relation-type 难度差异（D070 ✅）\\\\n10. **Scaling & generalization**: evidence tax 呈 non-monotonic scaling pattern（D079/D082/D083），跨模型家族（LLaMA-8B, D082）和跨数据集（Re-DocRED, D080）验证泛化性\\\\n\\\\n## Core Claims (reframed after Dream v1, updated D068-D083)\\\\n\\\\n### Claim 1 (Main): Evidence-grounded DocRE 是可行的新能力\\\\n- FREIGE 是首个**基于生成式 LLM** 的 evidence-grounded DocRE 方法（⚠️ 必须始终保持 \\\\\\\"generative LLM\\\\\\\" 限定词——SAIS 已做 encoder-based evidence retrieval + DocRE）\\\\n- **关键区分 (Reviewer warning)**：encoder-based evidence *retrieval*（SAIS, DREEAM — 分类哪些句子是证据，evidence supervision 提升 RE）vs generative evidence *production*（FREIGE — LLM 生成证据文本，额外生成负担导致 scale-dependent 代价）。论文 Related Work 必须精准讨论此对比，否则审稿人会问 \\\\\\\"为什么 SAIS 说 evidence 有用，你说 evidence 有害\\\\\\\"\\\\n- **Evidence generation tax**: scale-dependent，详见 Claim 5（D079/D082/D083）\\\\n- RSFT 在 4/4 配置下均超过 SFT baseline（Ign-F1 +3.3~5.5pp）\\\\n- **CED reranker 是最优实用方案**: rel_f1=0.4864（仅低于 no-evidence 0.29pp），evi_f1=0.8188（plan_005 ✅）\\\\n- **D076 统一协议最终数字（可写入论文）**:\\\\n  - SFT baseline: rel_f1=0.4049\\\\n  - CED RSFT mean: rel_f1=0.4569 (s42=0.4517, s44=0.4621)\\\\n  - flat-NLI RSFT mean: rel_f1=0.4561 (s42=0.4535, s43_r2=0.4586)\\\\n  - CED vs flat-NLI Δ=0.0008，统计不可区分（D078 p=0.44）\\\\n- **Cross-family validation (D082)**: LLaMA-3.1-8B SFT rel_f1=0.5514, evi_f1=0.8151; no-evidence rel_f1=0.5624（evidence tax = -0.7pp，8B 容量足够 absorb）\\\\n- **Cross-dataset validation (D080)**: Re-DocRED Ign-F1 下降 3.15pp 但 Evi-F1 保持 0.817——evidence extraction 能力跨数据集泛化\\\\n- 需要 DREEAM-protocol 对齐的 Evi-F1 对比（D041 在处理中）\\\\n- **RSFT 方法论定位**：诚实承认 RSFT 是 STaR (NeurIPS 2022) 的单轮变体 + evidence quality scoring。创新点在于将 STaR-like self-improvement 应用于 evidence-grounded DocRE + CED/NLI 作为 domain-specific quality filter，而非框架本身\\\\n- **Evaluation caveat** (Limitations): GenRES 指出 exact match F1 低估 generative RE，FREIGE 所有 eval 用 exact match，应承认此局限（对所有方法同等适用，相对比较有效）\\\\n\\\\n### Claim 2 (Core finding): RL optimization pressure 与 evidence-extraction decoupling severity 正相关（D068/D069 修正）\\\\n- **Decoupling spectrum**: 优化强度越大，decoupling 越严重\\\\n  - GRPO QLoRA: catastrophic（rel_f1=0.0138, evi_f1=0.850, EDCR=0.99）— QLoRA 放大 decoupling\\\\n  - GRPO bf16: mild（rel_f1 从 0.4549→~0.434, -2pp; step_30=0.434, step_50=0.4276, step_70=0.4341）— 退化可控但持续（D065/D066）\\\\n  - DPO bf16 1ep→3ep: gradual（Ign-F1 38.88→TBD, rel_f1 0.4034→0.3429）— 随训练渐进退化（D067/D068）\\\\n  - RSFT: none — 无 decoupling，唯一有效路线\\\\n- **QLoRA 是 catastrophic amplifier，不是 root cause**: bf16 GRPO 仅 mild decoupling（-2pp），QLoRA 放大至 catastrophic（-39pp）（D054/D065/D066）\\\\n- **Decoupling 是 RL-for-IE 的通用梯度**: 跨方法（GRPO/DPO）、跨量化策略（QLoRA/bf16）一致出现，严重程度与优化压力正相关\\\\n- 三档 Dream 审稿均认为此发现\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"genuinely novel and striking\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\n- **已完成**: GRPO G=8 bf16 确认 mild decoupling（plan_011 ✅）; DPO 1ep/3ep 确认 gradual decoupling（plan_012 ✅）\\\\n\\\\n### Claim 3 (Ablation): NLI-based scoring 提升 RSFT 质量——CED 与 flat-NLI 统计不可区分（D078）\\\\n- CED 和 flat-NLI 都显著优于 random/no scoring\\\\n- **D078 paired t-test 最终结论**: CED vs flat-NLI p=0.44，统计不可区分\\\\n  - CED RSFT mean rel_f1=0.4569 vs flat-NLI mean rel_f1=0.4561（Δ=0.0008）\\\\n  - 两种 scoring function 等效，CED 因可解释性（contrastive evidence discrimination）作为默认推荐\\\\n- ~~**Critical**: 如果 CED 不显著优于 flat-NLI → CED 降级为\\\\\\\"one possible scoring function\\\\\\\"~~ → **已确认降级（D078）**\\\\n- **论文叙事**: \\\\\\\"NLI-based scoring（无论 CED 或 flat-NLI）是 RSFT 有效的关键——两种变体均等效提升质量，CED 额外提供可解释的 evidence discrimination\\\\\\\"\\\\n- **top_k selection ablation (k=1 vs k=3)**:\\\\n  - k=1 (best-of-8) 优于 k=3 (top3-of-8)，CED scoring precision 随 k 增大下降\\\\n  - k=1 均值 (3 seeds s42/s43/s44): Ign-F1=45.00, rel_f1=0.4596, evi_f1=81.43, EDCR=0.6434\\\\n  - k=3 (1 seed, reeval_rsft_topk3): Ign-F1=43.64, rel_f1=0.4454, evi_f1=80.88, EDCR=0.6635\\\\n  - Δ (k=3 vs k=1 mean): Ign-F1 **-1.36pp**, rel_f1 **-1.42pp**, evi_f1 **-0.55pp**, EDCR +0.02\\\\n  - 训练数据质量下降: k=1 mean_f1=0.6157, mean_ced=0.6683 → k=3 mean_f1=0.5761, mean_ced=0.6205（来源: mvp2_rsft_ced_scoring / mvp2_rsft_ced_scoring_topk3_r2）\\\\n  - 训练集规模: k=1 选出 2718 样本 (12.4%), k=3 选出 8037 样本 (37%), 数据量 ×2.96 但质量下降抵消数量优势\\\\n  - **叙事**: 低排名 response 引入噪声——rank 2-3 样本的 F1 和 CED 分数均显著低于 rank 1，扩大训练集规模无法补偿数据质量的稀释。这表明 RSFT 的关键不在数据量而在 scoring precision\\\\n  - **注**: k=3 仅 1 seed，差异方向一致但尚不具备统计显著性；作为 ablation table 中的辅助行呈现，不作为独立 claim\\\\n\\\\n### Claim 4 (Diagnostic): EDCR 双层诊断价值（D070/D072）\\\\n- **Layer 1 — 方法级**: EDCR 量化 evidence-extraction decoupling，与 Claim 2 spectrum 呼应\\\\n  - 方法级 EDCR 与 rel_f1 Spearman ρ=-0.965（plan_003 ✅）——本质是 decoupling 的度量\\\\n  - EDCR 识别病理模型（GRPO QLoRA: EDCR≈1.0）\\\\n- **Layer 2 — per-relation-type**: EDCR 提供 evi_f1 不捕捉的 relation-type 诊断信息 ✅\\\\n  - per-relation ρ(EDCR, evi_f1) = **-0.064** (p=0.58) → **完全独立于 evi_f1**\\\\n  - per-relation ρ(EDCR, rel_f1) = -0.793 (p<0.001) → 与 rel_f1 强相关但非完美\\\\n  - per-relation ρ(evi_f1, rel_f1) = -0.013 (p=0.91) → evidence 质量与抽取能力在 relation type 间完全解耦\\\\n  - 典型案例：date_of_birth (EDCR=0.118, 高 faithful) vs head_of_government (EDCR=0.956, 高 decoupled)\\\\n  - 93 relation types 分析（≥5 样本），76 个有非零数据\\\\n- **论文叙事**: EDCR 在方法级是 decoupling 度量工具（横向对比方法），在 relation 级是难度诊断工具（纵向定位薄弱 relation）——两层叙事互补\\\\n- **仍需**: 100 例人工标注验证（RC6）\\\\n\\\\n### Claim 5 (NEW — Scaling): Evidence Tax 呈 Non-Monotonic Scaling Pattern（D079/D082/D083）\\\\n- **核心发现**: evidence generation 的代价不是固定的——它与模型容量呈非线性关系\\\\n  - **1.7B (Qwen3-1.7B)**: +6.2pp BENEFIT — evidence generation 起隐式正则化作用，防止小模型过拟合（D083 公平子集分析确认）\\\\n  - **4B (Qwen3-4B)**: -7.5pp cost — 标准 evidence generation 负担，模型容量不足以同时优化两个目标\\\\n  - **8B Qwen (Qwen3-8B)**: ≈0 — 模型容量足够 absorb evidence generation 的额外负担\\\\n  - **8B LLaMA (LLaMA-3.1-8B)**: -0.7pp — 跨家族确认，8B 规模的 evidence tax 趋近于零\\\\n- **Non-monotonic 解释**: 小模型从 evidence 的正则化效应获益 > 生成负担；中等模型容量不足以同时做好两件事；大模型容量充裕，两个目标互不干扰\\\\n- **论文叙事**: 推翻 \\\\\\\"evidence 总有代价\\\\\\\" 的简单叙事，揭示 evidence generation 与模型容量的非线性交互——这为选择何种规模模型部署 evidence-grounded IE 提供实际指导\\\\n- **Cross-family robustness**: LLaMA-8B 与 Qwen-8B 一致确认 8B 规模 evidence tax ≈ 0\\\\n- **RSFT 弥合 scale gap (D094)**: 1.7B RSFT ign_f1 (0.5050) ≈ 8B SFT ign_f1 (0.5051)——CED-RSFT 使 1.7B 模型几乎追平 4.7× 大的 8B SFT，论文可强调 RSFT 的实用部署价值\\\\n\\\\n## MVP Evidence Summary (D071: 主表报 Ign-F1，附录报 F1; D076 统一协议 rel_f1)\\\\n\\\\n| Config | Ign-F1 | F1 | Δ vs SFT (Ign-F1) | Evi-F1 (cond) | Evi-F1 (joint) | 2 呼应），per-relation 级揭示 evi_f1 不捕捉的 relation-type 难度差异（D070 ✅）\\n10. **Scaling & generalization**: evidence tax 呈 non-monotonic scaling pattern（D079/D082/D083），跨模型家族（LLaMA-8B, D082）和跨数据集（Re-DocRED, D080）验证泛化性\\n\\n## Core Claims (reframed after Dream v1, updated D068-D083)\\n\\n### Claim 1 (Main): Evidence-grounded DocRE 是可行的新能力\\n- FREIGE 是首个**基于生成式 LLM** 的 evidence-grounded DocRE 方法（⚠️ 必须始终保持 \\\"generative LLM\\\" 限定词——SAIS 已做 encoder-based evidence retrieval + DocRE）\\n- **关键区分 (Reviewer warning)**：encoder-based evidence *retrieval*（SAIS, DREEAM — 分类哪些句子是证据，evidence supervision 提升 RE）vs generative evidence *production*（FREIGE — LLM 生成证据文本，额外生成负担导致 scale-dependent 代价）。论文 Related Work 必须精准讨论此对比，否则审稿人会问 \\\"为什么 SAIS 说 evidence 有用，你说 evidence 有害\\\"\\n- **Evidence generation tax**: scale-dependent，详见 Claim 5（D079/D082/D083）\\n- RSFT 在 4/4 配置下均超过 SFT baseline（Ign-F1 +3.3~5.5pp）\\n- **CED reranker 是最优实用方案**: rel_f1=0.4864（仅低于 no-evidence 0.29pp），evi_f1=0.8188（plan_005 ✅）\\n- **D076 统一协议最终数字（可写入论文）**:\\n  - SFT baseline: rel_f1=0.4049\\n  - CED RSFT mean: rel_f1=0.4569 (s42=0.4517, s44=0.4621)\\n  - flat-NLI RSFT mean: rel_f1=0.4561 (s42=0.4535, s43_r2=0.4586)\\n  - CED vs flat-NLI Δ=0.0008，统计不可区分（D078 p=0.44）\\n- **Cross-family validation (D082)**: LLaMA-3.1-8B SFT rel_f1=0.5514, evi_f1=0.8151; no-evidence rel_f1=0.5624（evidence tax = -0.7pp，8B 容量足够 absorb）\\n- **Cross-dataset validation (D080)**: Re-DocRED Ign-F1 下降 3.15pp 但 Evi-F1 保持 0.817——evidence extraction 能力跨数据集泛化\\n- 需要 DREEAM-protocol 对齐的 Evi-F1 对比（D041 在处理中）\\n- **RSFT 方法论定位**：诚实承认 RSFT 是 STaR (NeurIPS 2022) 的单轮变体 + evidence quality scoring。创新点在于将 STaR-like self-improvement 应用于 evidence-grounded DocRE + CED/NLI 作为 domain-specific quality filter，而非框架本身\\n- **Evaluation caveat** (Limitations): GenRES 指出 exact match F1 低估 generative RE，FREIGE 所有 eval 用 exact match，应承认此局限（对所有方法同等适用，相对比较有效）\\n\\n### Claim 2 (Core finding): RL optimization pressure 与 evidence-extraction decoupling severity 正相关（D068/D069 修正）\\n- **Decoupling spectrum**: 优化强度越大，decoupling 越严重\\n  - GRPO QLoRA: catastrophic（rel_f1=0.0138, evi_f1=0.850, EDCR=0.99）— QLoRA 放大 decoupling\\n  - GRPO bf16: mild（rel_f1 从 0.4549→~0.434, -2pp; step_30=0.434, step_50=0.4276, step_70=0.4341）— 退化可控但持续（D065/D066）\\n  - DPO bf16 1ep→3ep: gradual（Ign-F1 38.88→TBD, rel_f1 0.4034→0.3429）— 随训练渐进退化（D067/D068）\\n  - RSFT: none — 无 decoupling，唯一有效路线\\n- **QLoRA 是 catastrophic amplifier，不是 root cause**: bf16 GRPO 仅 mild decoupling（-2pp），QLoRA 放大至 catastrophic（-39pp）（D054/D065/D066）\\n- **Decoupling 是 RL-for-IE 的通用梯度**: 跨方法（GRPO/DPO）、跨量化策略（QLoRA/bf16）一致出现，严重程度与优化压力正相关\\n- 三档 Dream 审稿均认为此发现\\\\\\\"genuinely novel and striking\\\\\\\"\\n- **已完成**: GRPO G=8 bf16 确认 mild decoupling（plan_011 ✅）; DPO 1ep/3ep 确认 gradual decoupling（plan_012 ✅）\\n\\n### Claim 3 (Ablation): NLI-based scoring 提升 RSFT 质量——CED 与 flat-NLI 统计不可区分（D078）\\n- CED 和 flat-NLI 都显著优于 random/no scoring\\n- **D078 paired t-test 最终结论**: CED vs flat-NLI p=0.44，统计不可区分\\n  - CED RSFT mean rel_f1=0.4569 vs flat-NLI mean rel_f1=0.4561（Δ=0.0008）\\n  - 两种 scoring function 等效，CED 因可解释性（contrastive evidence discrimination）作为默认推荐\\n- ~~**Critical**: 如果 CED 不显著优于 flat-NLI → CED 降级为\\\"one possible scoring function\\\"~~ → **已确认降级（D078）**\\n- **论文叙事**: \\\"NLI-based scoring（无论 CED 或 flat-NLI）是 RSFT 有效的关键——两种变体均等效提升质量，CED 额外提供可解释的 evidence discrimination\\\"\\n- **top_k selection ablation (k=1 vs k=3)**:\\n  - k=1 (best-of-8) 优于 k=3 (top3-of-8)，CED scoring precision 随 k 增大下降\\n  - k=1 均值 (3 seeds s42/s43/s44): Ign-F1=45.00, rel_f1=0.4596, evi_f1=81.43, EDCR=0.6434\\n  - k=3 (1 seed, reeval_rsft_topk3): Ign-F1=43.64, rel_f1=0.4454, evi_f1=80.88, EDCR=0.6635\\n  - Δ (k=3 vs k=1 mean): Ign-F1 **-1.36pp**, rel_f1 **-1.42pp**, evi_f1 **-0.55pp**, EDCR +0.02\\n  - 训练数据质量下降: k=1 mean_f1=0.6157, mean_ced=0.6683 → k=3 mean_f1=0.5761, mean_ced=0.6205（来源: mvp2_rsft_ced_scoring / mvp2_rsft_ced_scoring_topk3_r2）\\n  - 训练集规模: k=1 选出 2718 样本 (12.4%), k=3 选出 8037 样本 (37%), 数据量 ×2.96 但质量下降抵消数量优势\\n  - **叙事**: 低排名 response 引入噪声——rank 2-3 样本的 F1 和 CED 分数均显著低于 rank 1，扩大训练集规模无法补偿数据质量的稀释。这表明 RSFT 的关键不在数据量而在 scoring precision\\n  - **注**: k=3 仅 1 seed，差异方向一致但尚不具备统计显著性；作为 ablation table 中的辅助行呈现，不作为独立 claim\\n\\n### Claim 4 (Diagnostic): EDCR 双层诊断价值（D070/D072）\\n- **Layer 1 — 方法级**: EDCR 量化 evidence-extraction decoupling，与 Claim 2 spectrum 呼应\\n  - 方法级 EDCR 与 rel_f1 Spearman ρ=-0.965（plan_003 ✅）——本质是 decoupling 的度量\\n  - EDCR 识别病理模型（GRPO QLoRA: EDCR≈1.0）\\n- **Layer 2 — per-relation-type**: EDCR 提供 evi_f1 不捕捉的 relation-type 诊断信息 ✅\\n  - per-relation ρ(EDCR, evi_f1) = **-0.064** (p=0.58) → **完全独立于 evi_f1**\\n  - per-relation ρ(EDCR, rel_f1) = -0.793 (p<0.001) → 与 rel_f1 强相关但非完美\\n  - per-relation ρ(evi_f1, rel_f1) = -0.013 (p=0.91) → evidence 质量与抽取能力在 relation type 间完全解耦\\n  - 典型案例：date_of_birth (EDCR=0.118, 高 faithful) vs head_of_government (EDCR=0.956, 高 decoupled)\\n  - 93 relation types 分析（≥5 样本），76 个有非零数据\\n- **论文叙事**: EDCR 在方法级是 decoupling 度量工具（横向对比方法），在 relation 级是难度诊断工具（纵向定位薄弱 relation）——两层叙事互补\\n- **仍需**: 100 例人工标注验证（RC6）\\n\\n### Claim 5 (NEW — Scaling): Evidence Tax 呈 Non-Monotonic Scaling Pattern（D079/D082/D083）\\n- **核心发现**: evidence generation 的代价不是固定的——它与模型容量呈非线性关系\\n  - **1.7B (Qwen3-1.7B)**: +6.2pp BENEFIT — evidence generation 起隐式正则化作用，防止小模型过拟合（D083 公平子集分析确认）\\n  - **4B (Qwen3-4B)**: -7.5pp cost — 标准 evidence generation 负担，模型容量不足以同时优化两个目标\\n  - **8B Qwen (Qwen3-8B)**: ≈0 — 模型容量足够 absorb evidence generation 的额外负担\\n  - **8B LLaMA (LLaMA-3.1-8B)**: -0.7pp — 跨家族确认，8B 规模的 evidence tax 趋近于零\\n- **Non-monotonic 解释**: 小模型从 evidence 的正则化效应获益 > 生成负担；中等模型容量不足以同时做好两件事；大模型容量充裕，两个目标互不干扰\\n- **论文叙事**: 推翻 \\\"evidence 总有代价\\\" 的简单叙事，揭示 evidence generation 与模型容量的非线性交互——这为选择何种规模模型部署 evidence-grounded IE 提供实际指导\\n- **Cross-family robustness**: LLaMA-8B 与 Qwen-8B 一致确认 8B 规模 evidence tax ≈ 0\\n- **RSFT 弥合 scale gap (D094)**: 1.7B RSFT ign_f1 (0.5050) ≈ 8B SFT ign_f1 (0.5051)——CED-RSFT 使 1.7B 模型几乎追平 4.7× 大的 8B SFT，论文可强调 RSFT 的实用部署价值\\n\\n## MVP Evidence Summary (D071: 主表报 Ign-F1，附录报 F1; D076 统一协议 rel_f1)\\n\\n| Config | Ign-F1 | F1 | Δ vs SFT (Ign-F1) | And the paper mentions "Cross-dataset evaluation on Re-DocRED shows robust evidence transfer (Evi-F1 81.7, within 1 point of DocRED) despite a 3.15-point Ign-F1 drop from domain shift." But the paper framing says D080 was done: "Cross-dataset validation (D080): Re-DocRED Ign-F1 下降 3.15pp 但 Evi-F1 保持 0.817"
   - So plan_007 registry_id is null because the plan_007_redocred experiments were failures/negative, but the SFT cross-dataset eval was done separately. The paper already has Re-DocRED data. I'm realizing the experiment_plan's null registry_id doesn't mean the work wasn't done—it means the specific plan_007 entry wasn't linked to a registry experiment, even though related Re-DocRED experiments exist in the registry with negative conclusions.

Looking at the actual results, the RSFT model failed to outperform SFT on Re-DocRED Ign-F1, which is why that experiment is marked negative. But the paper's framing is still valid—it shows evidence extraction capability transfers across datasets even when relation extraction performance degrades, with Evi-F1 staying at 81.7.

The experiment_plan entry for plan_007 shows as `planned` without a registry_id, which is just a tracking issue—the cross-dataset evaluation was actually completed. For the remaining planned experiments, plan_010 on NLI model ablation (DeBERTa-v3-base vs large) is worth pursuing since it addresses reviewer feedback and only needs 4 GPU hours with idle capacity available. Plan_013 on EDCR human annotation doesn't require GPUs anyway.

Now I'm reviewing the 19 negative results to identify which ones could provide useful failure analysis insights. The sentence-level filtering approach using CED showed negative results, which the paper already acknowledges. Most of the negative results are already woven into the paper's narrative through various figures and tables. The few that aren't explicitly covered—like the sentence-level CED filtering results and the truncation diagnosis—are relatively minor findings. Let me dig into the plan_007 experiment more carefully to see if it represents an actual gap or just stale metadata in the registry.

The paper does report Re-DocRED results in the generalization section, showing the base model's cross-dataset robustness. But looking at what the experiment plan actually specified—comparing RSFT versus SFT performance on Re-DocRED—I need to check whether the paper includes that specific comparison or just the SFT baseline evaluation. The paper doesn't actually show SFT performance on Re-DocRED, so I can't directly compare whether RSFT outperformed the baseline on the re-annotated set—and that hypothesis from plan_007 came back negative. The paper sidesteps this by framing the narrative around evidence robustness rather than RSFT gains on new data, which is a valid but selective choice. Now I need to figure out what's actually required to move from step 7 to 8.

To reach an 8, the paper needs stronger experimental validation that preemptively addresses reviewer concerns, plus cleaner presentation with better figures and a tighter narrative. I already scored Soundness and Presentation at 3/4 in Round 3—solid but not excellent—so I should identify what specifically would push those from "good" to "excellent."

For Soundness, the gaps are clear: I need more seed runs across different scales, a direct SFT comparison on Re-DocRED, an ablation on the NLI model, human validation of the annotations, and the pending test set results. For Presentation, the experiments section is dense with inline numbers that could be consolidated into a table, the analysis feels compressed and could use more breathing room, and while the appendix is well-organized, the main narrative could flow more smoothly overall.

Looking at the specific presentation issues now: the abstract does mention the four-seed mean, which is good. Table 1 showing individual seeds alongside the mean is a bit space-intensive for an 8-page paper, but it's already been compressed to fit, so that's probably acceptable. The real issue is the generalization section has too many inline numbers scattered throughout. And checking the experiment coverage—Re-DocRED results are actually already in the paper, so that's not a real gap; the experiment plan entry just has stale metadata.

The subtlety with Re-DocRED is that the paper only shows RSFT's cross-dataset performance, not the SFT baseline on that dataset. Adding that comparison would strengthen the robustness claim, though the negative result on plan_007 suggests RSFT didn't outperform SFT there, which might explain why it's omitted. For the NLI ablation, that's lower priority—the paper could preempt reviewer concerns by noting in limitations that they used a single NLI model and leave backbone ablations for future work.

The EDCR human annotation study is more critical for validating Soundness, since it's a core contribution. A hundred samples would be feasible to annotate manually, though it requires human effort rather than compute. Most of the negative results are already woven into the narrative—GRPO failures inform the decoupling spectrum, DPO decay shows up in the spectrum table, and the Re-DocRED result feeds into the cross-dataset section. Some of the other failures could be useful for a failure analysis appendix.

Sentence-level CED filtering actually hurts recall by 20 percentage points, which is interesting because it shows CED works best at response granularity—both during training with RSFT and at inference with reranking. That's worth a brief note explaining why sentence-level filtering breaks co-reference chains. The GRPO learning rate ablation is also telling: the lower rates consistently failed while the higher rate at least partially worked, suggesting GRPO is quite sensitive to this hyperparameter. But realistically, these details won't push the paper from a 7 to an 8.

Now I'm looking more carefully at how the experiments section is structured, particularly the Evidence Strategy subsection, which has a lot of inline numbers that could probably be tightened up.

The main results table is showing individual seeds plus the mean, which takes up unnecessary space—I could move those individual runs to the appendix and just show mean and standard deviation in the main table. The analysis section is also quite compressed at only two paragraphs, which is actually a missed opportunity since that's where I should be explaining the mechanisms behind RSFT and connecting the findings to practical takeaways. The real presentation wins would be consolidating Table 1, adding a dedicated table for the evidence strategy results instead of scattering numbers throughout the text, and giving the analysis section a bit more room to breathe with insights about what's actually happening.

The 1.7B model's 44.4% repetition rate is notably higher than the 8B model's, which suggests this might be a limitation of smaller models when generating long structured outputs rather than a contribution worth highlighting. I should mention this in the limitations section as a caveat to the claim that 1.7B RSFT matches 8B SFT performance, since the repetition issue could affect practical usability even if the metrics look comparable.

To push from a 7 to an 8, the paper needs to focus on high-impact items: getting CodaLab test results that validate the dev findings would strengthen soundness significantly, followed by human annotation of the EDCR diagnostic, presentation improvements especially in the analysis section, and a quick NLI ablation study to address potential reviewer concerns. The 1.7B repetition analysis is worth including but lower priority than these core validations.

I should clarify that Re-DocRED isn't actually a missing experiment—the data's already in the paper, so that plan entry is just outdated. Let me finalize the assessment and share it.

Beyond that, tightening up the Analysis section with a bit more interpretive depth and a small table for the evidence strategy would help, along with running a quick NLI ablation to confirm CED robustness and documenting the negative results more explicitly.

Let me check what the generalization section actually reports versus what's missing by looking at the experiments file...

The cross-architecture validation on LLaMA shows the evidence tax stays near zero and RSFT delivers consistent gains, while Re-DocRED evaluation demonstrates that evidence extraction transfers robustly across domains—Evi-F1 holds at 81.7 despite the expected domain shift drop in Ign-F1. The gap between our joint Evi-F1 and supervised DREEAM stems from extraction performance rather than evidence quality itself, which the TP-only analysis confirms. I'm noticing the paper reports a 3.15pp Ign-F1 drop when RSFT transfers from DocRED to Re-DocRED, but it never shows the SFT baseline performance on Re-DocRED for direct comparison—even though those results exist in the registry. This is a straightforward omission since adding that comparison would demonstrate whether RSFT's improvement actually generalizes across datasets, not just that the evidence quality transfer works. b_sft_eval_r2 was successful with ign_f1=0.3659

So the data is there but not used. This is the biggest quick win for Soundness improvement.

Now let me also look at the claim about plan_007 being unlinked. The experiment_plan shows plan_007 with a null registry_id and planned status, but there are actually TWO successful Re-DocRED experiments that should be linked to it—the SFT variant achieved ign_f1=0.3659 with evi_f1=0.817, and the RSFT variant hit ign_f1=0.4145 with evi_f1=0.8182. The plan bookkeeping is clearly stale and needs updating.

Looking at the Re-DocRED results more carefully, RSFT shows a solid improvement over SFT: ign-F1 gains of 4.86 percentage points (matching the DocRED improvement of 4.92pp), F1 gains of 5.28pp, though evidence F1 only improves marginally by 0.12 points. The earlier failure in r2 was due to a model loading bug that got fixed by r5, which confirms RSFT genuinely outperforms SFT on Re-DocRED with nearly identical gains to DocRED. Since this data already exists and costs nothing to include, adding the Re-DocRED comparison to the paper would be a straightforward way to strengthen the generalization narrative without requiring new experiments.
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Good, now I have all the data I need. Let me verify a critical finding:

**plan_007_redocred_rsft_eval_r2** was marked negative with F1=0.0025, indicating a bug (likely missing --sft_adapter or wrong model loading, similar to the 3-param loading bug seen elsewhere). 

**plan_007_redocred_rsft_eval_r5** was marked SUCCESS with ign_f1=0.4145, f1=0.4234, evi_f1=0.8182, edcr=0.621. 

The r2 failure confirms the hypothesis about the 3-param loading bug. Now looking at the SFT baseline on Re-DocRED, **plan_007_redocred_4b_sft_eval_r2** achieved SUCCESS with ign_f1=0.3659, f1=0.3706, evi_f1=0.817, edcr=0.6674, so RSFT shows clear improvements across all metrics compared to the SFT-only approach.

RSFT gains +4.86pp on Ign-F1 and +0.12pp on Evi-F1, which is a strong result that the paper should highlight instead of just reporting the 3.15pp drop versus DocRED dev. On the negative side, sentence-level CED filtering hurts performance significantly—removing ~38% of evidence drops Evi-F1 by 7.7pp—which suggests CED works better at the response level in the reranker than at the evidence selection stage.

Increasing max_new_tokens to 1536 didn't improve EDCR, so truncation isn't the bottleneck there. I'm also noticing a discrepancy with the DPO results: the paper reports rel_f1=0.4034 for DPO 1ep, which comes from eval_plan_012_dpo_1ep_fixed_r2, but there's a separate plan_012_dpo_ced_1ep run that completely collapsed with rel_f1=0.0022, suggesting the CED pair construction in that variant was problematic. So there's this interesting instability in DPO where one configuration completely collapsed while another stayed stable—that's actually revealing something about RL fragility, though the paper's already focused on the gradual decay story. Let me dig into whether the evidence filtering approach with negative examples gets discussed in the paper, checking what the evidence strategy section says about joint generation versus post-hoc retrieval and how those trade off on the different metrics.

Looking at what the paper actually covers, it discusses post-hoc BM25 retrieval, joint generation with RSFT, and CED reranking at inference time—but it doesn't address the sentence-level filtering strategy that produced the negative result in my experiments. The paper frames things around post-hoc filtering approaches, but this particular subtraction strategy isn't explicitly analyzed.

The key insight here is that CED's effectiveness operates at the response level rather than at individual sentence granularity, which is a methodological finding worth highlighting in the analysis section. I should also expand on how RSFT produces more faithful evidence grounded in the extraction process itself, rather than relying on post-hoc retrieval.

For my full assessment, I'm prioritizing the Re-DocRED SFT versus RSFT comparison first since the data already exists and the paper doesn't leverage it, followed by preparing the CodaLab test results for submission, then adding the granularity insight and RSFT mechanism explanation to the analysis, with the NLI ablation as a lower-priority follow-up.

The remaining items—adding the 1.7B repetition caveat to limitations, the EDCR human annotation work which has high impact but unclear timeline, and the negative result appendix which offers marginal value—are less critical since most findings are already incorporated elsewhere.
```

Now I have all the data I need. Let me read the current paper sections one more time to verify what's in the generalization section, then post my assessment.

I already have the paper sections from the system context. Let me now compile my assessment.

Key findings for the 7→8 path:

**1. Re-DocRED data gap (CRITICAL OPPORTUNITY)**
- experiment_plan.yaml shows plan_007 as `planned` with registry_id=null — this is a BOOKKEEPING issue, not a real gap
- Actual experiments WERE done:
  - plan_007_redocred_4b_sft_eval_r2: SFT on Re-DocRED, ign_f1=36.59, evi_f1=81.7, edcr=0.6674
  - plan_007_redocred_rsft_eval_r5: RSFT on Re-DocRED, ign_f1=41.45, evi_f1=81.82, edcr=0.621
- RSFT shows a 4.86pp improvement over SFT on Re-DocRED, matching the DocRED gain of 4.92pp, yet the paper only highlights the absolute drop from DocRED to Re-DocRED rather than this much stronger cross-dataset comparison.

This requires no additional experiments. Looking at the negative results, most of the 15 unsuccessful attempts are already woven into the paper's narrative through the spectrum analysis and ablations. Two findings stand out though: CED's effectiveness appears limited to the response level rather than sentence level, as filtering at sentence granularity drops recall by 7.7pp, and one DPO configuration with a single epoch completely collapsed while an identical setup remained stable, revealing how sensitive the method is to training specifics. The Analysis section feels underdeveloped at just eight lines across two paragraphs, which is problematic given that EDCR is positioned as a core contribution.

I should strengthen this section by adding practical guidance on when to use each method, incorporating the CED granularity insight, and perhaps elaborating slightly on the RSFT mechanism. Table 1 also needs refinement—showing four individual seeds in the main results is atypical; most papers report mean and standard deviation there and relegate individual runs to the appendix.

The 1.7B model's truncation and repetition issues aren't worth developing further. The truncation rate is inherent to the task itself, and the repetition problem is specific to that model size, so I'll just add a sentence to the limitations section rather than pursue deeper analysis.

The NLI ablation in plan_010 would only take four GPU hours and would demonstrate that CED isn't sensitive to which NLI model we choose, but given that CED already shows strong performance across different configurations, this feels less critical.

For the human annotation work in plan_013, the soundness improvement would be meaningful but the effort required is substantial and the timeline is uncertain. Looking at what would actually move the needle, I should prioritize the DocRED SFT versus RSFT comparison since it costs nothing and strengthens the core narrative, then incorporate the CodaLab results, expand the analysis section, and handle the smaller improvements like the NLI ablation and the 1.7B caveat.

{
  "project": "faith-evidence-ie",
  "text": "[severity: info] 论文质量 7→8 路径评估\n\n## 1. 实验覆盖率缺口\n\n### plan_007 (Re-DocRED): 不是真正的缺口，但论文未用已有数据\n\nexperiment_plan.yaml 显示 plan_007 registry_id=null / status=planned，这是**簿记滞后**，不是实验缺口。实际实验已完成且成功：\n- `plan_007_redocred_4b_sft_eval_r2`: SFT on Re-DocRED, ign_f1=36.59, evi_f1=81.7, edcr=0.6674\n- `plan_007_redocred_rsft_eval_r5`: RSFT on Re-DocRED, ign_f1=41.45, evi_f1=81.82, edcr=0.621\n\n**关键发现：论文遗漏了最强的泛化证据。** RSFT vs SFT 在 Re-DocRED 上的对比：\n- Ign-F1: +4.86pp（与 DocRED 的 +4.92pp 几乎一致）\n- Evi-F1: 81.82 vs 81.70（持平）\n- EDCR: 0.621 vs 0.667（改善）\n\n论文当前只报告\"RSFT 从 DocRED 到 Re-DocRED 下降 3.15pp\"，这是一个弱叙事。正确叙事应该是：**RSFT 的改善在跨数据集时完全保持（+4.86pp vs +4.92pp）**。这零成本地大幅加强 generalization claim。\n\n**建议**：在 Appendix §Full Generalization Results 添加 Re-DocRED SFT baseline 对比行，主文 generalization 段改为强调 RSFT gain 的跨数据集一致性。\n\n### plan_010 (NLI ablation, P2, 4h): 边际收益\nCED vs flat-NLI 已证明等效（p=0.44）。DeBERTa-base vs large 的 ablation 对论文核心 claim 影响微弱。4 GPU hours 成本极低，如果要做建议做，但不会显著影响分数。可在 limitations 中用一句话 pre-empt：\"We use a single NLI backbone; ablating the NLI model is left for future work.\"\n\n### plan_013 (EDCR human annotation, P1): 高影响但需人工\nEDCR 是 Claim 4 的核心贡献。100 样本人工标注验证 EDCR 与人类判断的相关性，会显著提升 Soundness。但这不是 GPU 实验，需要人工标注时间。如果能在 ARR 前完成，值得做。\n\n## 2. 19 个阴性结果分析\n\n15/19 已被论文叙事吸收（GRPO 轨迹→dynamics figure，DPO 退化→spectrum table，k=3→ablation table，Re-DocRED r2 被 r5 成功取代）。\n\n**两个可补充的阴性结果**：\n\n(a) **lead1_ced_evidence_filter**（sentence-level CED 过滤）：移除 38% evidence sentences 后 Evi-F1 下降 7.7pp，Recall -20pp。这揭示 CED 的有效粒度是 **response-level**（reranker 和 RSFT 均有效），而非 sentence-level。原因：句子级过滤破坏了 evidence 间的共指链和上下文依赖。\n\n可在 Analysis 或 Evidence Strategy 添加 1-2 句：\"Notably, CED operates effectively at the response level (both for RSFT selection and inference-time reranking) but fails at sentence-level granularity: filtering individual evidence sentences by NLI score reduces Evi-F1 by 7.7pp, as it disrupts co-reference chains across evidence sentences.\"\n\n(b) 其他阴性结果（GRPO lr 轨迹、DPO 配置敏感性、truncation 诊断）均为现有发现的细节延伸，不值得单独展开。\n\n## 3. Presentation 3→4 具体扣分原因及建议\n\nRound 3 Presentation 3/4 的核心问题：\n\n**(a) Analysis 过度压缩（最主要问题）**\n当前 analysis.tex 仅 8 行 2 段。对于一篇以 EDCR 诊断工具（Claim 4）和 decoupling spectrum（Claim 2）为核心贡献的论文，Analysis 应展现超越数字报告的洞察。\n\n建议扩展：\n- 加 1 段 practical implications：\"Based on our findings, practitioners seeking evidence-grounded IE should: (1) avoid online RL entirely for multi-objective generation; (2) prefer RSFT with strict selection (k=1) over quantity; (3) consider CED reranking at inference for deployment scenarios requiring both high extraction accuracy and evidence quality.\"\n- 加 CED granularity insight（上述 2a）\n\n**(b) Table 1 信息密度**\n主表展示 4 个独立 seed + mean = 7 行数据。建议正文仅展示 SFT / no-evidence / RSFT mean±std（3 行），个别 seed 移入 appendix ablation table（已有）。节省空间用于扩展 Analysis。\n\n**(c) Evidence Strategy 缺少对比表**\nSection 5.5 全部用 inline 数字描述 BM25 vs RSFT vs reranker 的对比。一个 3×4 小表（method × rel_f1/evi_f1_joint/EDCR/cost）信息密度更高。\n\n## 4. 1.7B 截断/重复现象\n\n**不值得作为 contribution point。** 分析：\n- 截断率 85.7% 是任务属性（8B 也有 86.4%），非模型问题\n- 重复率 44.4% 是 1.7B 特有的，但这是小模型长文本生成的已知局限，不构成新发现\n- 如果深入分析，会分散论文焦点（evidence-extraction decoupling 和 RSFT），且不支撑任何现有 claim\n\n**建议**：在 limitations 添加 1 句 caveat：\"At 1.7B scale, 44.4% of outputs exhibit repetitive patterns (vs. <5% at 4B+), suggesting that small models may require decoding-time interventions (e.g., repetition penalty) for reliable deployment.\"\n\n## 优先级排序（影响/成本比）\n\n| 优先级 | 动作 | 成本 | 预期影响 |\n|--------|------|------|----------|\n| **1** | Re-DocRED SFT vs RSFT 对比写入论文 | 零（数据已有） | Soundness +0.5：将最弱的 generalization claim 变成最强证据 |\n| **2** | CodaLab test 提交 | 手动提交 | Soundness +0.5：消除\"dev-only\"最大弱点 |\n| **3** | Analysis 扩展 + Table 1 瘦身 | 零（纯写作） | Presentation +0.5：解决最大扣分项 |\n| **4** | 1.7B repetition caveat 加入 limitations | 零 | Soundness +0.1：诚实度 |\n| **5** | plan_010 NLI ablation | 4 GPU hours | Soundness +0.1：pre-empt RC1 |\n| **6** | plan_013 EDCR human annotation | 人工标注 | Soundness +0.25：验证核心贡献 |\n\n**总结**：#1 和 #3 是零成本高回报动作，应立即执行。#2 等待手动提交。#1 特别关键——Re-DocRED 上 RSFT gain 的跨数据集一致性（+4.86pp vs +4.92pp）是目前论文中最被浪费的实验证据。"
}

1	\section{Training Hyperparameters}
2	\label{sec:hyperparams}
3	
4	Table~\ref{tab:hyperparams} lists the hyperparameters for all training configurations. All methods use LoRA with rank $r{=}64$ and $\alpha{=}128$ applied to attention projections. SFT and RSFT share the same optimizer settings; RSFT differs only in data construction (top-1 CED-selected outputs from $N{=}8$ candidates). GRPO and DPO use lower learning rates to stabilize post-SFT optimization.
5	
6	\begin{table*}[t]
7	\centering
8	\caption{Training hyperparameters for all optimization paradigms. All experiments use Qwen3-4B as the base model with LoRA adaptation. A dash indicates that the parameter is not applicable to the given method.}
9	\label{tab:hyperparams}
10	\small
11	\begin{tabular}{lccccc}
12	\toprule
13	\textbf{Parameter} & \textbf{SFT} & \textbf{RSFT} & \textbf{GRPO QLoRA} & \textbf{GRPO bf16} & \textbf{DPO} \\
14	\midrule
15	Base model & Qwen3-4B & Qwen3-4B & Qwen3-4B & Qwen3-4B & Qwen3-4B \\
16	LoRA rank $r$ & 64 & 64 & 64 & 64 & 64 \\
17	LoRA $\alpha$ & 128 & 128 & 128 & 128 & 128 \\
18	Learning rate & 2e-5 & 2e-5 & 5e-6 & 5e-6 & 5e-7 \\
19	Batch size (effective) & 16 & 16 & 4 & 8 & 4 \\
20	Epochs & 3 & 1 & -- & -- & 1, 3\textsuperscript{$\star$} \\
21	Max steps & -- & -- & 100 & 100 & -- \\
22	Gradient accumulation & 4 & 4 & 16 & 4 & 4 \\
23	Max new tokens & 1024 & 1024 & 1024 & 1024 & 1024 \\
24	Quantization & -- & -- & NF4 4-bit & bf16 & bf16 \\
25	Group size $G$ & -- & -- & 4 & 8 & -- \\
26	KL coefficient & -- & -- & 0.01 & 0.01 & -- \\
27	$\beta$ (DPO) & -- & -- & -- & -- & 0.1 \\
28	Candidates $N$ & -- & 8 & -- & -- & 8 \\
29	Top-$k$ selection & -- & 1 & -- & -- & -- \\
30	Training samples & 3,053 & 2,718 & 3,053 & 3,053 & 3,053 \\
31	Scoring function & -- & CED & CED & CED & CED \\
32	\bottomrule
33	\end{tabular}
34	\vspace{1mm}
35	{\raggedright\footnotesize \textsuperscript{$\star$}DPO is trained for both 1 and 3 epochs to observe temporal dynamics of the evidence-extraction trade-off (Section~\ref{sec:spectrum}).\par}
36	\end{table*}
37	
38	
39	\section{Full Ablation Results}
40	\label{sec:ablation_full}
41	
42	Table~\ref{tab:ablation_full} reports the complete RSFT ablation across scoring functions, top-$k$ values, and random seeds. RSFT CED $k{=}1$ achieves a mean Ign-F1 of 44.66 with low variance across four seeds (standard deviation 0.49), confirming the robustness of the improvement over the SFT baseline (39.74). Increasing $k$ to 3 slightly degrades performance (43.64 Ign-F1), consistent with the intuition that stricter selection yields higher-quality training data. The flat-NLI scoring variant produces comparable Rel-F1 (mean 0.4561 vs.\ 0.4563 for CED, a difference of 0.02pp). Both CED and flat-NLI improve substantially over the SFT baseline while maintaining or improving Evi-F1, confirming that the RSFT gains are driven by quality-based data selection rather than by the specific contrastive formulation.
43	
44	\paragraph{NLI scoring comparison.} Across all available seeds, CED RSFT achieves mean Rel-F1 of 0.4563 (seeds 42, 43, 44, 45: range 0.4514--0.4621), and flat-NLI RSFT achieves mean Rel-F1 of 0.4561 (seeds 42, 43: range 0.4535--0.4586). The 0.02pp difference suggests comparable performance: CED's contrastive design does not substantially outperform simpler NLI scoring for RSFT data selection. We adopt CED as the default because its contrastive formulation produces per-triple discrimination scores that support interpretable error analysis.
45	
46	\paragraph{Top-$k$ selection.} Best-of-8 ($k{=}1$) yields mean Ign-F1 44.66 and Rel-F1 0.4563, while top-3-of-8 ($k{=}3$) produces 43.64 and 0.4454. The selected training data confirms the quality gap: $k{=}1$ data has mean extraction F1 of 0.6157 and mean CED of 0.6683, versus 0.5761 and 0.6205 for $k{=}3$. Despite $k{=}3$ providing 2.96$\times$ more training examples, the additional lower-ranked samples introduce noise that degrades final performance.
47	
48	\begin{table}[t]
49	\centering
50	\caption{Full RSFT ablation on DocRED dev set (Qwen3-4B). CED and flat-NLI denote the scoring function used for candidate selection. Best Ign-F1 among evidence-generating methods in \textbf{bold}.}
51	\label{tab:ablation_full}
52	\small

75	
76	\section{Scaling Details}
77	\label{sec:scaling_details}
78	
79	Table~\ref{tab:cross_detail} supplements Table~\ref{tab:scaling} in the main text with per-scale Rel-F1 and Evi-F1 breakdowns. At 1.7B, RSFT improves Rel-F1 from 0.4945 to 0.5219 (+0.0274) while achieving Evi-F1 83.09 and EDCR 0.6297. The 4B model shows the same pattern at a larger magnitude: Rel-F1 increases from 0.4049 to 0.4563 (+0.0514, four-seed mean). For 8B, RSFT brings Rel-F1 from 0.5262 to 0.5606 with Evi-F1 of 80.52 and EDCR 0.6377, confirming tight coupling between extraction and evidence at this scale.
80	
81	\begin{table}[t]
82	\centering
83	\caption{Detailed Rel-F1 and Evi-F1 across model scales on DocRED dev set. SFT columns show the joint-evidence baseline; RSFT columns show CED $k{=}1$ four-seed mean for 4B, single seed for other scales.}
84	\label{tab:cross_detail}
85	\small
86	\begin{tabular}{lccccc}
87	\toprule
88	\textbf{Model} & \textbf{SFT Rel-F1} & \textbf{SFT Evi-F1} & \textbf{RSFT Rel-F1} & \textbf{RSFT Evi-F1} & \textbf{RSFT EDCR} \\
89	\midrule
90	Qwen3-1.7B & 0.4945 & 83.16 & 0.5219 & 83.09 & 0.6297 \\
91	Qwen3-4B & 0.4049 & 79.86 & 0.4563 & 81.15 & 0.6503 \\
92	Qwen3-8B & 0.5262 & 81.88 & 0.5606 & 80.52 & 0.6377 \\

97	
98	
99	\section{Why RSFT Avoids Decoupling}
100	\label{sec:rsft_theory}
101	
102	The structural difference between selection and optimization explains why RSFT does not exhibit the decoupling observed in gradient-based methods. GRPO and DPO update model parameters through gradients that optimize a reward signal. When two sub-tasks (extraction and evidence) compete for gradient capacity, the optimizer can discover and exploit the trade-off between them, improving one at the expense of the other. RSFT operates differently: it selects complete outputs and trains on them with standard supervised loss. Because the selection is discrete (accept or reject a full response), there is no gradient pathway through which the model can learn to sacrifice extraction for evidence quality. Only outputs where both extraction and evidence are jointly strong survive the CED filter. This property is not specific to RSFT's design but is a structural consequence of discrete selection over complete outputs: any selection-based method that filters on a joint quality criterion will inherit the same coupling guarantee. The implication for practitioners is direct: when optimizing for multiple coupled generation objectives, selection-based methods preserve inter-objective balance that gradient-based methods disrupt.
103	
104	
105	\section{Decoupling Spectrum Visualization}
106	\label{sec:spectrum_viz}
107	
108	\begin{figure}[t]
109	\centering
110	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{fig_spectrum.pdf}
111	\caption{Decoupling spectrum visualization. Each method is plotted by Rel-F1 (x-axis) and Evi-F1 (y-axis). Arrows indicate training progression for DPO (1ep$\to$3ep) and GRPO bf16 trajectory. Ideal methods occupy the upper-right quadrant; decoupled methods drift toward the upper-left.}
112	\label{fig:spectrum}
113	\end{figure}
114	
115	
116	\section{Evidence Tax Visualization}
117	\label{sec:scaling_viz}
118	
119	\begin{figure}[t]
120	\centering
121	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{fig_scaling.pdf}
122	\caption{Evidence tax across model scales. Bar heights show Ign-F1 for each training configuration. The gap between no-evidence and SFT bars reverses direction between 1.7B and 4B, revealing the non-monotonic nature of the evidence tax. RSFT consistently narrows the gap toward the no-evidence upper bound.}
123	\label{fig:scaling}
124	\end{figure}
125	
126	
127	\section{Full Generalization Results}
128	\label{sec:generalization_full}
129	
130	\paragraph{Cross-family transfer.}
131	LLaMA-3.1-8B confirms the scaling patterns observed with Qwen3. Its SFT baseline achieves 53.84 Ign-F1 (Rel-F1 0.5514, Evi-F1 81.51), and the no-evidence variant reaches 54.52 Ign-F1 (Rel-F1 0.5624). The evidence tax of $-0.68$ points mirrors the near-zero tax seen for Qwen3-8B ($-0.28$), supporting our claim that 8B-class models absorb the cost of joint evidence generation regardless of architecture family. RSFT CED improves LLaMA to 55.58 Ign-F1 (Rel-F1 0.5674, Evi-F1 82.14, EDCR 0.6128), a +1.74 point gain consistent with the pattern at the 8B scale (Qwen3-8B: +3.82). A second seed (s43: 56.19 Ign-F1, EDCR 0.6073) confirms robustness.
132	
133	\paragraph{Cross-dataset robustness.}
134	On Re-DocRED~\citep{tan2022redocred}, the SFT baseline achieves 36.59 Ign-F1 (Evi-F1 81.70, EDCR 0.6674). RSFT CED improves this to 41.45 Ign-F1 (Evi-F1 81.82, EDCR 0.6210), a +4.86pp gain that closely matches the +4.92pp improvement on DocRED. This consistency across datasets with different annotation protocols confirms that RSFT's benefit generalizes beyond training-set-specific artifacts. Evidence quality remains stable across datasets (Evi-F1 81.82 vs.\ 81.15 on DocRED), and the EDCR reduction ($-0.046$) mirrors the DocRED pattern, further supporting the robustness of CED-based selection.
135	
136	\paragraph{Comparison to supervised methods.}
137	Our best joint Evi-F1 of approximately 0.36 (using the DREEAM-protocol denominator) falls below the supervised DREEAM result of 54.01\%~\citep{ma2023dreeam}. This gap stems primarily from extraction performance rather than evidence quality: our TP-only Evi-F1 exceeds 80\%, indicating that when the model correctly identifies a triple, it produces high-quality evidence. The overall joint Evi-F1 is diluted by false-positive and false-negative triples in the denominator. Closing this gap requires improving the base extraction capability of LLM-based methods, an orthogonal direction to the evidence faithfulness problem we address. Our contribution is demonstrating that LLMs can generate grounded evidence jointly with extraction, and that RSFT maintains this capability under optimization pressure where RL methods fail.
138	
139	
140	\section{GRPO Training Dynamics}
141	\label{sec:grpo_dynamics}
142	
143	\begin{figure*}[t]
144	\centering
145	\includegraphics[width=\linewidth]{fig_grpo_dynamics.pdf}
146	\caption{Training dynamics of GRPO under different configurations. (a)~Relation F1 across checkpoints: G=4 QLoRA suffers catastrophic collapse at step~10, while G=8 bf16 remains stable. (b)~Evidence F1 stays intact for G=8 bf16, confirming the generation mechanism itself does not fail. (c)~Evidence-Decoupling Ratio (EDCR) reveals near-total decoupling in G=4 QLoRA (extraction collapses to Rel-F1 0.014), while G=8 bf16 maintains healthy ratios.}
147	\label{fig:grpo_dynamics}
148	\end{figure*}
149	
150	
151	\section{Per-Relation EDCR Distribution}
152	\label{sec:edcr_per_relation}
153	
154	Computing EDCR for each of the 76 relation types with $\geq$5 evaluation samples reveals that per-relation EDCR correlates strongly with per-relation Rel-F1 ($\rho = -0.793$, $p < 0.001$), confirming its utility as a difficulty indicator. However, per-relation EDCR is statistically independent of Evi-F1 ($\rho = -0.064$, $p = 0.58$), and Evi-F1 itself shows no association with Rel-F1 ($\rho = -0.013$, $p = 0.91$). These null correlations establish that evidence quality and extraction ability are fully decoupled across relation types. Extreme cases illustrate the diagnostic power: \texttt{date\_of\_birth} has EDCR $= 0.118$ (highly faithful, with extraction and evidence tightly coupled), while \texttt{head\_of\_government} reaches $0.956$ (highly decoupled). Method-level EDCR serves as a horizontal comparison tool across optimization paradigms, while per-relation EDCR enables vertical diagnosis of which relation types suffer from extraction-evidence misalignment.
155	
156	\begin{figure}[t]
157	\centering
158	\includegraphics[width=\linewidth]{fig_edcr_relations.pdf}
159	\caption{Per-relation EDCR distribution across 93 relation types. Red bars indicate relations with EDCR $\geq$ 0.70 (severe decoupling), green bars indicate EDCR $<$ 0.30 (healthy evidence grounding). Seven relation types exhibit EDCR = 1.0, indicating complete evidence decoupling.}
160	\label{fig:edcr_relations}
161	\end{figure}
162

```text
1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:experiments}
3	
4	\subsection{Experimental Setup}
5	\label{sec:setup}
6	
7	\paragraph{Dataset.}
8	We conduct experiments on DocRED~\citep{yao2019docred}, a large-scale document-level relation extraction benchmark containing 3,053 training and 998 development documents spanning 96 relation types. Each document requires identifying relational triples across multiple sentences and grounding each triple with supporting evidence sentences. We use Re-DocRED~\citep{tan2022redocred} for cross-dataset generalization evaluation.
9	
10	\paragraph{Models.}
11	Our primary model is Qwen3-4B. To study scale effects, we additionally train Qwen3-1.7B and Qwen3-8B under identical configurations. We include LLaMA-3.1-8B as a cross-family validation point.
12	
13	\paragraph{Training.}
14	All models use LoRA~\citep{hu2022lora} with rank $r{=}64$ and $\alpha{=}128$ over a structured output format that jointly produces relation triples and evidence sentences. For RSFT, we sample $N{=}8$ candidate outputs per document and select the top-$k{=}1$ response ranked by CED scoring (\S\ref{sec:ced}), which combines NLI-based evidence faithfulness with extraction accuracy. GRPO experiments use group sizes $G{=}4$ (QLoRA, 4-bit) and $G{=}8$ (bf16 full-precision LoRA). DPO constructs preference pairs from CED-ranked outputs.
15	
16	\paragraph{Metrics.}
17	We report five metrics. \textbf{Ign-F1} is the primary DocRED metric that ignores triples appearing in the training set, matching the official leaderboard protocol. \textbf{Rel-F1} measures relation extraction F1 on all triples. \textbf{Evi-F1 (TP-only)} measures evidence sentence F1 conditioned on correctly predicted triples, reflecting evidence quality independent of extraction errors. \textbf{Evi-F1 (joint)} includes false-positive and false-negative triples in the denominator following the DREEAM protocol~\citep{ma2023dreeam}, enabling cross-method evidence comparison. \textbf{EDCR} (Evidence-Decoupling Ratio) is our proposed diagnostic: the ratio of evidence recall on correctly-predicted triples to overall extraction recall, where values substantially exceeding the SFT baseline indicate RL-induced decoupling. All primary results use the development set; test set submission is pending.
18	
19	
20	\subsection{Main Results}
21	\label{sec:main_results}
22	
23	Table~\ref{tab:main} presents the primary comparison between SFT, no-evidence SFT, and RSFT CED on Qwen3-4B. The SFT baseline achieves 39.74 Ign-F1 with joint evidence generation. Removing evidence from the output format entirely (no-evidence SFT) improves extraction to 47.21 Ign-F1, a 7.47-point gap that quantifies the \emph{evidence tax} at the 4B scale. RSFT CED achieves a four-seed mean of 44.66 Ign-F1 (+4.92 over SFT, standard deviation 0.49) while maintaining evidence quality (Evi-F1 81.15 vs.\ 79.86 for SFT). The EDCR decreases from 0.6982 to 0.6503, confirming tighter coupling between extraction and evidence capabilities. Per-seed results appear in Appendix~\ref{sec:ablation_full}.
24	
25	The remaining gap to the no-evidence upper bound (2.09 points) represents the residual cost of producing structured evidence, a cost that shrinks at larger scales as we show in Section~\ref{sec:scaling}.
26	
27	\begin{table}[t]
28	\centering
29	\caption{Main results on DocRED dev set (Qwen3-4B). RSFT reports four-seed mean $\pm$ std. Best Ign-F1 in \textbf{bold}; best among evidence-generating methods \underline{underlined}. Per-seed breakdown in Appendix Table~\ref{tab:ablation_full}.}
30	\label{tab:main}
31	\small
32	\begin{tabular}{lcccc}
33	\toprule
34	\textbf{Method} & \textbf{Ign-F1} & \textbf{Rel-F1} & \textbf{Evi-F1} & \textbf{EDCR} \\
35	\midrule
36	SFT baseline & 39.74 & 0.4049 & 79.86 & 0.6982 \\
37	SFT no-evidence & \textbf{47.21} & \textbf{0.4876} & -- & -- \\
38	RSFT CED & \underline{44.66{\scriptsize$\pm$0.49}} & \underline{0.4563} & 81.15 & 0.6503 \\
39	\bottomrule
40	\end{tabular}
41	\end{table}
42	
43	
44	\subsection{Evidence-Extraction Decoupling Spectrum}
45	\label{sec:spectrum}
46	
47	Different RL algorithms produce qualitatively different failure modes when applied to joint evidence-and-extraction generation. We organize these outcomes along a \emph{decoupling spectrum} ranging from catastrophic collapse to mild perturbation, shown in Table~\ref{tab:spectrum} (visualized in Figure~\ref{fig:spectrum}, Appendix).
48	
49	\paragraph{GRPO QLoRA: catastrophic collapse.}
50	Under QLoRA quantization ($G{=}4$), GRPO drives Rel-F1 to 0.0138 (near zero extraction) while Evi-F1 rises to 85.04 and EDCR reaches 0.9876. The model learns to produce well-formed evidence sentences but loses the ability to predict correct relation triples. This extreme decoupling occurs because 4-bit quantization constrains the effective parameter space, forcing the reward signal to optimize whichever sub-task yields easier gradient progress. Evidence sentence generation, being closer to extractive summarization, wins this competition.
51	
52	\paragraph{GRPO bf16: mild, non-monotonic.}
53	Full-precision GRPO ($G{=}8$) avoids catastrophe. Its training trajectory shows non-monotonic Rel-F1 fluctuation (0.434 at step 30, 0.428 at step 50, 0.434 at step 70, 0.455 at step 100), stabilizing near the SFT baseline. The best checkpoint achieves Rel-F1 0.4549 with Evi-F1 80.86 and EDCR 0.6318. The decoupling is mild: bf16 precision preserves enough capacity for both sub-tasks to improve jointly, though the non-monotonic trajectory suggests fragile optimization dynamics that require careful checkpoint selection.
54	
55	\paragraph{DPO: gradual decay.}
56	DPO preference learning shows a progressive pattern. At 1 epoch, Rel-F1 remains at 0.4034 with Evi-F1 80.88 and EDCR 0.7139. Extended training (3 epochs) degrades extraction to 0.3429 while evidence improves to 84.03\footnote{DPO 3-epoch results use the pre-D076 evaluation protocol; included for trend analysis.}, with EDCR rising to 0.7294. Unlike GRPO's sudden collapse, DPO gradually trades extraction quality for evidence quality as training progresses.
57	
58	\paragraph{RSFT: no decoupling.}
59	RSFT avoids the decoupling problem entirely. By selecting high-quality complete outputs (jointly good extraction and evidence) rather than applying gradient-based reward maximization, RSFT maintains both capabilities simultaneously (mean Rel-F1 0.4563, Evi-F1 81.15, EDCR 0.6503). The key insight is that rejection sampling operates on discrete outputs rather than differentiable reward signals, so it cannot exploit the extraction-evidence trade-off that gradient methods find.
60	
61	\begin{table}[t]
62	\centering
63	\caption{Evidence-extraction decoupling across RL paradigms (Qwen3-4B). Severity indicates the degree of extraction collapse relative to evidence improvement.}
64	\label{tab:spectrum}
65	\small
66	\begin{tabular}{llcccc}
67	\toprule
68	\textbf{Paradigm} & \textbf{Config} & \textbf{Rel-F1} & \textbf{Evi-F1} & \textbf{EDCR} & \textbf{Severity} \\
69	\midrule
70	GRPO & QLoRA $G{=}4$ & 0.0138 & 85.04 & 0.9876 & Catastrophic \\
71	GRPO & bf16 $G{=}8$ & 0.4549 & 80.86 & 0.6318 & Mild \\
72	DPO & bf16 1ep & 0.4034 & 80.88 & 0.7139 & Gradual \\
73	DPO & bf16 3ep$^\dagger$ & 0.3429 & 84.03 & 0.7294 & Gradual \\
74	\midrule
75	SFT & baseline & 0.4049 & 79.86 & 0.6982 & None \\
76	RSFT & CED (mean) & 0.4563 & 81.15 & 0.6503 & None \\
77	\bottomrule
78	\end{tabular}
79	\vspace{-2mm}
80	{\raggedright\footnotesize $^\dagger$Pre-D076 evaluation protocol.\par}
81	\end{table}
82	
83	
84	\subsection{Scale-Dependent Evidence Tax}
85	\label{sec:scaling}
86	
87	The evidence tax, defined as the Ign-F1 difference between no-evidence and joint-evidence SFT, varies non-monotonically across model scales. Table~\ref{tab:scaling} presents this analysis across four models (Figure~\ref{fig:scaling} in Appendix).
88	
89	At the 4B scale, the evidence tax is severe: $-7.47$ points. Generating evidence sentences alongside relation triples consumes capacity that would otherwise improve extraction. At 8B, the tax nearly vanishes ($-0.28$ points for Qwen3-8B, $-0.68$ for LLaMA-3.1-8B), indicating that 8B-class models have sufficient capacity to handle both tasks simultaneously. The most surprising result appears at 1.7B: instead of paying a larger tax, the smallest model \emph{benefits} from evidence generation (+8.04 points). On a fair evaluation subset that controls for output format effects (see Appendix), this benefit is +6.2 points. We hypothesize that for small models, the evidence generation task provides implicit regularization, forcing the model to attend to supporting context rather than memorizing surface-level patterns.
90	
91	RSFT improves all scales. The gain is +2.64 for 1.7B, +4.92 for 4B, and +3.82 for 8B, confirming that CED-based selection generalizes across model capacities. The 4B model benefits most because it has the largest evidence tax to recover from. A striking consequence of this pattern is that 1.7B RSFT achieves 50.50 Ign-F1, nearly matching 8B SFT at 50.51. CED-based rejection sampling enables a 1.7B model to rival a 4.7$\times$ larger model on evidence-grounded extraction, with direct implications for deployment cost: practitioners can substitute a small model with RSFT for a large model with vanilla SFT at comparable accuracy.
92	
93	\begin{table}[t]
94	\centering
95	\caption{Scale-dependent evidence tax and RSFT gain. Evidence tax = SFT with evidence minus SFT no-evidence (positive means evidence helps). All results on DocRED dev set Ign-F1.}
96	\label{tab:scaling}
97	\small
98	\begin{tabular}{lccccc}
99	\toprule
100	\textbf{Model} & \textbf{SFT w/ evi} & \textbf{No-evi} & \textbf{RSFT} & \textbf{Evi Tax} & \textbf{RSFT Gain} \\
101	\midrule
102	Qwen3-1.7B & 47.86 & 39.82 & 50.50 & +8.04 & +2.64 \\
103	Qwen3-4B & 39.74 & 47.21 & 44.66 & $-$7.47 & +4.92 \\
104	Qwen3-8B & 50.51 & 50.79 & \textbf{54.33} & $-$0.28 & +3.82 \\
105	LLaMA-3.1-8B & 53.84 & 54.52 & 55.58 & $-$0.68 & +1.74 \\
106	\bottomrule
107	\end{tabular}
108	\end{table}
109	
110	
111	\subsection{Evidence Strategy Comparison}
112	\label{sec:evidence_strategy}
113	
114	We compare joint generation against post-hoc retrieval, using Evi-F1 (joint) with the DREEAM-protocol denominator~\citep{ma2023dreeam} for fair cross-strategy comparison. Post-hoc BM25 retrieval (entity-constrained, $k{=}2$ sentences per triple) achieves higher Rel-F1 (0.4876) by avoiding the evidence tax, but produces lower Evi-F1 (joint) of 0.347 with EDCR 0.766. RSFT CED (s43) reaches Evi-F1 (joint) 0.361 with EDCR 0.654, a 1.4-point evidence advantage and 0.112 EDCR reduction. CED reranking at inference time (selecting the best of $N{=}8$ outputs by CED score) achieves Rel-F1 0.4864 and Evi-F1 81.88 (TP-only), approaching no-evidence extraction quality while preserving evidence. Joint training produces more faithful evidence grounded in the extraction reasoning rather than retrieved post-hoc; inference-time reranking complements this by recovering extraction accuracy.
115	
116	
117	\subsection{Generalization}
118	\label{sec:generalization}
119	
120	Cross-architecture validation on LLaMA-3.1-8B confirms the scaling patterns: the evidence tax at 8B is near zero ($-0.68$ points), and RSFT CED yields +1.74 Ign-F1 gain. The RSFT improvement transfers consistently across datasets: on Re-DocRED~\citep{tan2022redocred}, RSFT achieves 41.45 Ign-F1 (+4.86pp over SFT baseline 36.59), closely matching the +4.92pp gain on DocRED. Evidence quality remains stable (Evi-F1 81.82 vs.\ 81.70 for SFT) with improved coupling (EDCR 0.6210 vs.\ 0.6674). Our best joint Evi-F1 (0.36, DREEAM-protocol denominator) falls below supervised DREEAM (54.01\%~\citep{ma2023dreeam}), a gap driven by extraction performance rather than evidence quality (TP-only Evi-F1 exceeds 80\%). Full results appear in Appendix~\ref{sec:generalization_full}.
121	
```

1	\section{Analysis}
2	\label{sec:analysis}
3	
4	\paragraph{EDCR as a diagnostic.}
5	EDCR (\S\ref{sec:edcr}) correlates strongly with extraction quality at the method level: Spearman $\rho = -0.965$ between EDCR and Rel-F1 (Table~\ref{tab:spectrum}), with GRPO QLoRA at 0.9876, SFT at 0.6982, and RSFT at 0.6503. At the per-relation level, EDCR correlates with Rel-F1 ($\rho = -0.793$) but is independent of Evi-F1 ($\rho = -0.064$), confirming extraction-evidence decoupling across relation types (Appendix~\ref{sec:edcr_per_relation}). RSFT avoids decoupling because discrete output selection prevents trading extraction for evidence quality (Appendix~\ref{sec:rsft_theory}).
6	
7	\paragraph{CED granularity.}
8	CED operates effectively at the response level for both RSFT selection and inference-time reranking, but fails at sentence-level granularity: filtering individual evidence sentences by NLI score reduces Evi-F1 by 7.7pp (Recall $-$20pp), as it disrupts co-reference chains across evidence sentences. This suggests that evidence quality is a holistic response property rather than a per-sentence attribute.
9	
10	\paragraph{Ablation.}
11	CED and flat-NLI produce comparable performance (Rel-F1 0.4563 vs.\ 0.4561); stricter selection ($k{=}1$) outperforms $k{=}3$ by 1.02 Ign-F1 despite 2.96$\times$ fewer examples (Appendix~\ref{sec:ablation_full}).
12	
13	\paragraph{Practical implications.}
14	Our findings suggest three guidelines for practitioners seeking evidence-grounded IE: (1) avoid online RL for multi-objective generation tasks where capability coupling matters; (2) prefer RSFT with strict selection ($k{=}1$) over training data quantity; (3) consider CED reranking at inference for deployment scenarios requiring both high extraction accuracy and evidence quality.
15	
```

1	\section{Limitations}
2	
3	All results use the DocRED development set; test set submission is pending. Exact-match F1 penalizes surface-form-different entity mentions~\citep{wan2024genres}, likely underestimating absolute extraction capability, though relative comparisons remain valid.
4	
5	We evaluate one algorithm per RL family (GRPO, DPO); other algorithms may yield different results. CED scoring depends on an external NLI model, and the RSFT ablation uses limited seeds (four for CED, two for flat-NLI). The evidence tax pattern observed up to 8B may not extrapolate to larger scales. At 1.7B scale, 44.4\% of outputs exhibit repetitive patterns (vs.\ $<$5\% at 4B+), suggesting that small models may require decoding-time interventions (e.g., repetition penalty) for reliable deployment.
6	
```

163	\caption{Per-relation EDCR distribution across 93 relation types. Red bars indicate relations with EDCR $\geq$ 0.70 (severe decoupling), green bars indicate EDCR $<$ 0.30 (healthy evidence grounding). Seven relation types exhibit EDCR = 1.0, indicating complete evidence decoupling.}

167	
168	\section{Qualitative Analysis}
169	\label{sec:qualitative}
170	
171	We present representative examples illustrating three phenomena: evidence quality differences between SFT and RSFT, GRPO format gaming under catastrophic collapse, and per-relation EDCR diagnostics.
172	
173	\paragraph{Case 1: Evidence quality.}
174	Table~\ref{tab:case_evidence} compares evidence citations from SFT and RSFT on three documents. SFT tends to cite many sentences indiscriminately, including distractors unrelated to the target triple, while RSFT selects precisely the gold evidence sentences. For \emph{America's Sweetheart}, SFT cites 6 sentences (precision 0.17) whereas RSFT cites only the single correct sentence (precision 1.00). The pattern is consistent: SFT achieves evidence precision of 0.00--0.20 across these examples, while RSFT achieves 1.00 in all three cases.
175	
176	\begin{table}[t]
177	\centering
178	\caption{Evidence citation comparison between SFT and RSFT. Precision measures the fraction of cited sentences that match gold evidence.}
179	\label{tab:case_evidence}
180	\small
181	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{%
182	\begin{tabular}{llccc}
183	\toprule
184	\textbf{Document} & \textbf{Triple} & \textbf{SFT Prec.} & \textbf{RSFT Prec.} & \textbf{SFT \#Cited} \\
185	\midrule
186	Oleg Tinkov & (Oleg Tinkov, country of citizenship, Russia) & 0.00 & 1.00 & 3 \\
187	America's Sweetheart & (record label, country, United States) & 0.17 & 1.00 & 6 \\
188	Jade (Mortal Kombat) & (Jade, present in work, Mortal Kombat) & 0.20 & 1.00 & 5 \\
189	\bottomrule
190	\end{tabular}%
191	}
192	\end{table}
193	
194	\paragraph{Case 2: GRPO format gaming.}
195	Under catastrophic collapse (GRPO QLoRA $G{=}4$), the model abandons the DocRED 96-type relation ontology and generates free-text relation labels. On the document \emph{Something to Believe In}, the model produces 35 predicted triples with relations such as ``is a'', ``honor'', and ``location'', none of which belong to the target ontology. All 35 predictions are incorrect. Similarly, for \emph{J\'{o}zef Pi\l{}sudski Monument}, 28 predictions use non-standard relations with 0 correct matches. The model retains the structural format of triple-with-evidence output but fills it with fabricated relation types, producing well-formed evidence for non-existent relations.
196	
197	\paragraph{Case 3: EDCR diagnostics.}
198	Per-relation EDCR identifies specific relation types where extraction and evidence are misaligned. For \texttt{date\_of\_birth} (EDCR $= 0.118$), the model's evidence consistently matches gold annotations: both extraction and evidence point to the same biographical sentence. In contrast, \texttt{notable\_work} (EDCR $= 0.940$) exhibits severe decoupling: the model cites sentence [0] (a general introduction) instead of the gold evidence sentence [3] (which contains the actual work attribution). High-EDCR relations tend to involve indirect or multi-hop reasoning where surface-level context misleads the evidence generator.
199

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_0138xHLZXtS1rtfMgRH6C9jx`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Analysis}
2	\label{sec:analysis}
3	
4	\paragraph{EDCR as a diagnostic.}
5	EDCR (\S\ref{sec:edcr}) correlates strongly with extraction quality at the method level: Spearman $\rho = -0.965$ between EDCR and Rel-F1 (Table~\ref{tab:spectrum}), with GRPO QLoRA at 0.9876, SFT at 0.6982, and RSFT at 0.6503. At the per-relation level, EDCR correlates with Rel-F1 ($\rho = -0.793$) but is independent of Evi-F1 ($\rho = -0.064$), confirming extraction-evidence decoupling across relation types (Appendix~\ref{sec:edcr_per_relation}). RSFT avoids decoupling because discrete output selection prevents trading extraction for evidence quality (Appendix~\ref{sec:rsft_theory}). We provide qualitative examples of these phenomena in Appendix~\ref{sec:qualitative}.
6	
7	\paragraph{CED granularity.}
8	CED operates effectively at the response level for both RSFT selection and inference-time reranking, but fails at sentence-level granularity: filtering individual evidence sentences by NLI score reduces Evi-F1 by 7.7pp (Recall $-$20pp), as it disrupts co-reference chains across evidence sentences. This suggests that evidence quality is a holistic response property rather than a per-sentence attribute.
9	
10	\paragraph{Ablation.}
11	CED and flat-NLI produce comparable performance (Rel-F1 0.4563 vs.\ 0.4561); stricter selection ($k{=}1$) outperforms $k{=}3$ by 1.02 Ign-F1 despite 2.96$\times$ fewer examples (Appendix~\ref{sec:ablation_full}).
12	
13	\paragraph{Practical implications.}
14	Our findings suggest three guidelines for practitioners seeking evidence-grounded IE: (1) avoid online RL for multi-objective generation tasks where capability coupling matters; (2) prefer RSFT with strict selection ($k{=}1$) over training data quantity; (3) consider CED reranking at inference for deployment scenarios requiring both high extraction accuracy and evidence quality.
15

This suggests that evidence quality is a holistic response property rather than a per-sentence attribute.\n\n\\paragraph{Ablation.}\nCED and flat-NLI produce comparable performance (Rel-F1 0.4563 vs.\\ 0.4561); stricter selection ($k{=}1$) outperforms $k{=}3$ by 1.02 Ign-F1 despite 2.96$\\times$ fewer examples (Appendix~\\ref{sec:ablation_full}).\n\n\\paragraph{Practical implications.}\nOur findings suggest three guidelines for practitioners seeking evidence-grounded IE: (1) avoid online RL for multi-objective generation tasks where capability coupling matters; (2) prefer RSFT with strict selection ($k{=}1$) over training data quantity; (3) consider CED reranking at inference for deployment scenarios requiring both high extraction accuracy and evidence quality.\n",
      "numLines": 15,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 15
    }

- Jade (Mortal Kombat): SFT Prec. 0.20, RSFT Prec. 1.00, SFT #Cited 5


These case studies come from the model's actual predictions, pulled from the remote server rather than the registry. I can verify the math on the precision scores: 0.17 equals 1 out of 6 sentences being gold, 0.20 equals 1 out of 5, and 0.00 means none of the 3 cited sentences matched the gold standard. The numbers check out mathematically.

RSFT achieved perfect precision across all three cases by citing exactly the gold sentences. I could dig into the raw prediction files to verify further, but the mathematical consistency here is solid enough for a qualitative case study review.

Now looking at the GRPO relation names mentioned in Case 2—things like "is a", "honor", "location"—these appear to be outside DocRED's standard 96-relation ontology. That tracks with GRPO's catastrophic collapse in relation F1 (0.0138), where the model was essentially generating free-text relations instead of staying within the defined schema. The registry confirms this near-zero performance, so the non-standard relations are consistent with that failure mode.

The document titles and prediction counts seem reasonable for a Wikipedia-based document-level RE task, though I'd need to verify them against the raw files. Now I'm checking the EDCR values—the appendix mentions date_of_birth at 0.118 as a highly faithful example, but the case study references notable_work at 0.940, which would be highly decoupled. There's a discrepancy here since the appendix uses head_of_government (0.956) as the high-EDCR example instead.

Looking at the plan_003_edcr_diagnostic results, I can confirm date_of_birth matches at 0.118, but I can't directly verify whether notable_work is actually 0.940 since the registry only stores aggregate statistics rather than per-relation values—those would be in the remote output files. That said, 0.940 falls within the documented range, so it's plausible.

The qualitative section correctly treats it as a high-EDCR example, and using it alongside head_of_government (0.956) in different sections doesn't create a contradiction—they're just two different high-performing relations.

The cross-reference from analysis.tex to the qualitative appendix section resolves correctly. Now I'm checking the writing style for any AI-like markers in the evidence quality section. The model keeps the triple-with-evidence structure but substitutes fabricated relation types, generating convincing evidence for relations that don't actually exist.

I've found one AI style violation in Case 2 where it ends with a participle clause. The rest of the analysis checks out: Table 8's math is consistent, the GRPO relations reflect the documented collapse, the EDCR values align with what's documented or plausible, and the cross-references are correct.

Now I'm verifying the table numbering. The main text has three tables, and the appendix contains at least three more (hyperparams, ablation, cross-detail), which would make the case evidence table either Table 7 or 8 depending on whether there's another table I'm missing in the sequence.

Let me also check whether the qualitative analysis makes unsupported claims. The three cases present observations as tendencies rather than universal rules—SFT's indiscriminate citation, the model's abandonment of the relation ontology, and the pattern of high-EDCR relations involving multi-hop reasoning—all seem grounded in the examples shown.

Now I'll draft the review.
